## PROJECT EVIDENCE — packets/MOVEMENT-002.md

# Movement brief — The North Yard

- PWA book id: `kindled-book-02-meridian`
- Series id: `kindled`
- Author: Opus 5.5
- Seat: Monroe Jackson 1.3.0 / O'Connor
- Chapters: 9–16
- Intended movement scale: approximately 48,000–55,000 words. Preserve full
  scenes, instruction, recovery, humor, and the Sag-line action. Do not pad to
  reach a quota and do not compress later chapters to fit one response.
- POV: Callie, Jab, and Toren remain three co-protagonists. This movement may
  lean toward Toren, but Callie and Jab must remain causally load-bearing and
  receive developed interior scenes. Rotate cleanly; do not head-hop.

## Where we enter

Movement One ends on D+10. Tonk's pin-site fever is being treated and the bone
looks clear only on a poor image. Senna has completed a slow, uncertain marrow
cycle and needs another at D+21. Tilda's medicine helps the headache but not the
growth or its right-side and language effects. Callie, Jab, Rook, and Toren face
the Director's office on D+13; Jab's separate review is D+14; Tonk's two-week
picture is D+16. The treatment machine is drifting, hand-corrected, and without
a spare. All three households remain conditional.

Read the eight canonical chapters together before writing forward. Carry every
body, clock, object, debt, promise, and knowledge boundary in
`provenance/MOVEMENT-001-CONTINUITY.md`. Chapter 8's final image—the single
yellow light waking slowly—is the movement seam. Do not recap Movement One in
summary; let consequences enter through what people have to do next.

## What this movement is for

Turn three conditionally admitted households into the beginnings of a defender
cohort and turn Meridian's north yard into a real teaching place. The station's
need and bureaucracy should force action, but the pleasure engine is learning:
three young people attempt the same practical problems through different doors,
compare results, fail visibly, correct one another, and begin to understand why
their differences form a team.

Use the D+13/D+14 reviews to establish terms, leverage, and consent. Meridian
recognizes Jab as a scarce medical recruit without owning him. Callie must not
be reduced to ground-reading labor; Toren must not inherit command merely
because he has led before. Rook, Katori, Pell, and Dee Wren disagree honestly
about safe training and institutional use. No adult is a cardboard villain.

Dee Wren is existing canon: the fourteen-year-old Homura survivor Rook walked
south for two months after the station fell eleven years ago, making Dee about
twenty-five now. Dee is the cross-medical practitioner the owner requested,
with access and control across more doors than the young protagonists. Do not
state an exact door count or complete personal history. The reunion with Rook
must carry the human weight of two people who survived the same destruction
and have not become the same kind of healer. Dee knows Homura's repeatable
capacity protocol; Rook knows embodied rescue under real pressure. Neither
invalidates the other.

The movement must dramatize rather than merely explain:

- The north yard's first three practical lanes: mixed-door field work,
  Resilience/body discipline, and Ward/Sustain medicine. Dessa's ledger and
  Senna's oral memory become working records, planting the future schools
  without naming a formal academy.
- Ash ladder stages 1–4: distinguish source-power from clean ash; measure
  personal reserve; absorb only a small known amount; let it settle without
  immediately spending it. Use objects, bodies, mistakes, and demonstrations.
- A same-obstacle/three-answers sequence. Jab forms the stable fish-knife metal
  and deliberately lights its working edge. Toren must retain his own ugly
  spike and attempt its light rather than copying Jab's shape. Callie's hook is
  already a curved blade expression; she works on her mobile personal Ward and
  its handoff to Edge rather than receiving an unearned straight sword.
- Toren's first deliberate attempt to hold Stride rigid as Guard. It fails, but
  leaves a concrete correction he can try again. Guard is structural Ember
  hardware angled to take a hit, not Ward and not a new door.
- Callie's first supervised attempt to widen her body-hugging Ward toward
  another person. Let the limitation be comprehensible and costly.
- Jab learning that his danger-sense, body support, and the warmth Tonk felt are
  one Sustain domain. Rook identifies the lived instinct; Dee names and tests
  the repeatable parts. This is recognition and early control, not mastery.
- Tonk's D+16 image and infection progression, Tilda's uneven response and at
  least one choice that preserves her authority, Senna's increasing loss of
  sensation and D+21 machine cycle, and honest recovery/setback for Rook, Wyck,
  Dessa, Toren, and Callie. No body resets between scenes.
- Warmth and humor inside the work: Tonk remains four; Senna remains sharp;
  Callie counts; Jab does arithmetic; Toren notices load and position. Let the
  young people enjoy some part of becoming capable.

## The Sag-line failure

The movement's substantial action sequence is a supervised hold that becomes a
real failure, not a contrived unsupervised youth test. Establish terrain,
starting positions, the held line, ash stores, adult support distance, the
cohort's assigned tasks, and what counts as success before the failure begins.
Then let the problem change.

The line breaks or folds under direct pressure. An adult or trainee suffers a
specific, trackable injury. The opposition adapts; the young people must also
adapt. Callie, Jab, and Toren each contribute through their distinct current
limits. Jab senses the injury before he sees it and stabilizes the casualty,
then empties himself because he has not yet learned ash-funded working depth.
His act saves time, not every consequence. The sequence should be developed,
spatially legible, and long enough for tactics, cost, fear, and correction to
change—not a short proof-of-power vignette.

Afterward, Rook identifies what Jab did as Sustain. Dee demonstrates that clean,
settled ash could have funded part of the work and kept the healer functional.
Meridian's interest in recruiting Jab hardens. The lesson is not that the child
should have spent more of himself; it is that skill, reserves, rotation, and an
abort line keep the healer from becoming the next patient.

## What must remain true / withheld

- Public byline is Monroe Jackson. O'Connor remains internal.
- The owner overrides the seat's normal 87/13 single-lead allocation: Callie,
  Jab, and Toren are co-protagonists.
- Sustain can recall an active radiological burden later, but this movement
  does not yet complete radiation-recall training. It cannot reverse fixed
  damage, destroyed marrow, Tilda's growth, Tonk's structural injury, or
  Senna's fixed nerve damage.
- Working depth increases safe use of ash already held by the soul. It is not
  rank, a second storage organ, or limitless capacity. Forced intake can spill,
  create false fullness, or cause overdraw.
- Do not launch the Caul Hill expedition in this movement. Let the failing
  machine, medical clocks, worsening Sag, Dee's knowledge, and the cost of the
  line failure make the combined mission necessary.
- Do not pay off Toren's Guard, Callie's group-scale Ward, Jab's radiation
  recall, the Sustain-echo creature, the disk clue, or Cinder's remnant early.
- Preserve Milo's death and the group's grief; do not use grief as a single
  speech and declare it resolved.
- Introduce no new continuing named character. An unnamed Meridian-native
  trainee may participate, but the owner names continuing cast.
- No romance is assumed. Do not force a triangle or commitment.
- No copied wording, names, scene order, or proprietary terms from the Iron
  Prince research. Apply only the selected abstract numerical craft formula.
- Prose must read cleanly aloud: clear referents, controlled long sentences,
  punctuation that exposes hierarchy, and thought-level paragraph turns.

## What the author owns

Own all eight chapter boundaries, the exact POV sequence, the shape of the
reviews, how Dee reaches or is found within Meridian, the reunion, the training
problems, dialogue, humor, tactics, and the specific Sag-line casualty. Develop
scenes rather than servicing a checklist. Plans describe the change; the author
discovers how people create it.

Write chapters 9–16 as one continuous creative run. Do not stop for per-chapter
editing or scoring. If output limits intervene, save complete chapters and a
precise continuation checkpoint, then resume the same Opus session and movement.

## Where we leave pressure

Leave the north yard physically established with three lanes and a first real
ledger. All three protagonists have attempted distinct progression and learned
something usable, but none has mastered the needed expedition capability. Jab
is publicly valuable and institutionally targeted after the Sag-line failure.
The medical and machine clocks are worse, not forgotten. Rook and Dee have shown
why the team needs both instinct and protocol. By the closing scene, the reader
should understand why a Caul Hill part run and ash-rich rip operation are becoming
one unavoidable mission, without ending on a dry mission briefing.

## Repair notes (same-author repair, Movement One)

- Ch. 6 opens with an explicit rewind: "Back on the second day, the afternoon of Tonk's operation". Its card, bread and warm-stone scenes are D+2; the barrow, shed and step scenes are D+3.
- "Tuesdays" is Senna's line (ch. 3, the night Rook says he is staying); ch. 6 now attributes it to her.
- Tilda's count on D+2 is "eleven days before the fourteenth"; her household review and the joint Director's summons fall on the same day, D+13.
- Hallet's D-0 count: eight persons in the pen; seven went out through the north yard gate; Jab came in off the road.
- Hut adjacency: eleven (Toren), twelve (Jab), fourteen (Callie). Eleven and twelve are one door apart; eleven and fourteen are two.

## Object state — the two cords

- **The metal's neck cord:** short; the conduit hangs on it. Taken at the gate D-0, returned at the hatch; in the paper tray at every yellow line.
- **The long cord:** the twelve-foot sibling tether from Book One's road. Returned from the hatch on D+2 evening; Jab ties one end to his own left wrist and puts the other in Tonk's hand. From then on Tonk's end stays knotted at his wrist. When the ward turns Jab out at the tenth hour, he ties his end to the bed rail; when he returns, he moves it back to his own wrist (dramatized D+10, second hour). At the D+10 yellow line he unties it from himself and gives Tonk the loose end, which goes in with him ("It's not metal").

## Open clocks at exit

- Senna's next marrow cycle: D+21. The machine is hand-corrected, and each run is slower and less certain.
- Tonk's two-week infection picture: D+16. The pin-site infection is being treated; the bone looks clear on a poor picture ("I think").
- Callie's household review and the joint Director's summons (Callie, Jab, Rook and Toren): D+13. Jab's review: D+14.
- Jab has not answered the four-season guard contract offer.
- Rook has begun the four-season ash-lifting debt.
- Signs the Sag is worsening: the slab dropped this week, and the ash lifts "thinner."
- Planted only: Caul Hill (the source of Senna's exposure; "rooms full of those racks"), and Toren's line "Four hundred miles is only as far as he's looked."

## Bodies at exit

- Tilda: headache gone on the pills; one short fit; word slips recovered; right hand and leg unchanged. Refuses the number.
- Tonk: four pins and an external bar; top pin inflamed; febrile; still four, funny, and angry.
- Senna: both hands numb to texture by D+2 (earlier than Sowerby's line); walks only a few steps; course underway.
- Rook: stood one hour's Ward on D+9 against orders; hands shake afterward; reduced margin.
- Toren: broken left forearm (landed on it D-0), opened right palm, stitched leg. Ember warm and unchecked.
- Callie: healing ribs and head stitches; two fingers of reserve in the "well," unused.
- Jab: eleven stitches in his left forearm and six in his thigh. Sustain reserve partly refilled; it would not rise on D+9.
- Wyck: collarbone and wrist strapped (now stated on the page in chs. 1, 2, 5 and 6); the last two fingers of his right hand numb and not moving. Dessa: leg worse on slopes.

## Deliberately absent

The Homura medic, the Meridian-native trainee, cohort training, Guard, the group Ward, working-depth teaching, the Caul Hill mission, the disk clue, and any Cinder material. No new named characters: the day holder, the shed technician, and the man in the long coat remain unnamed.

## The Sag-line failure (D+25), as staged

- **Positions.**
  - South lip: Pell (whistle, a lent watch), Dessa (count), Wyck (not cleared), and the day holder in her hollow holding the sheet.
  - North lip: Rook, due to take the line at minute 20.
  - East track: Katori guarding; Callie and the weighbridge girl lowest, just above post 5; Otto and two yard lifters above them. Nobody lifts below post 5.
  - Toren runs jars. Jab stands in the west door. Ines is on the west rim above the pipe's west mouth with her bow.
  - Dee is at the yard gate, 200 paces back, by the Director's rule (objected to by D. W. and P.), with the kit, the stretcher crew, three graded jars of Flask, and a Handful already settled in her.
- **Success, as written:** the lift up in 40 minutes; nothing through the line; nobody below their floor; everybody out on three blasts.
- **The sequence:**
  1. Minute 18: warm iron at post 5 and two shadows (Toren); "It's going" (Callie). Katori moves the lifters ten paces up.
  2. Minute 21: the slab at 6 drops, the sheet folds at the east corner ("Weight!"), and husks surge up.
  3. Three blasts. Callie, Toren and the girl are cut off below the surge and cross the bottom west. The girl falls correctly (wrist). Toren drops the jar bag, and a Flask and more is lost.
  4. Jab lights his knife to draw the husks, lighting through the belly with no gutter.
  5. The hounds go for the dark, into the pipe. Callie reads the stopped water and wards the hook across the west mouth; she has nothing on her. Toren's light fences the arch.
  6. Jab feels the lean at forty yards, hands the door to Toren ("She's worse") and crosses dark.
  7. The day holder had been hit at the fold, unseen, and is holding one-handed ("Get back to your post, boy"). She collapses and the sheet goes. Rook takes the whole bottom alone, for 31 minutes.
  8. Jab holds her nine minutes and goes below his floor.
  9. The hounds come back east, to the dark. Toren arrives with Stride and fences them; Katori kills both.
  10. Dee arrives, having done the girl's wrist first ("nobody had the holder on the count"; Wyck: "where's the holder"), takes over on settled ash, and lifts Jab's hand off. Jab collapses.
- **Callie's part:** the hook held to her floor at 58 s. The hounds had gone quiet by about 30. Katori came down the rim and said "out".

## Object state

- **The copied plate:** Toren's coat pocket, on the other side from the stone.
- **Dessa's ledger:** Pell's spare, with a cracked spine.
  - The first page holds "Say it out loud. Even when it's awful. — M. F. On the wall."
  - It also holds the map, the D+25 page with its columns, "COUNT THE HOLDER", and Senna's why ("Because the ones who hold the line are the ones nobody watches").
- **Dee's book** is separate. It holds "Floor: a finger. Did not say yes."
- **Dee's brass one-minute sand-glass:** "the only thing I took off the wire that wasn't on my back".
- **The long cord:**
  - Since D+25 it has run from Tonk's wrist to the rail of the empty sixth bed on the right, in a knot a nurse tied twice.
  - Tonk has said nobody is to untie it.
  - Jab went home to twelve on D+28 without it.
- **The fish ("Little"):** it was on Jab's ward pillow on D+25–26; it is not tracked since.
- **Tonk's frame:** it now has a trapeze bar on a chain.
- **The weighbridge girl's tally board:** now on Tilda.

## Open clocks at exit (D+28)

- **Machine:**
  - The fourth box is drifting and a fifth box, next along, is now waking slow.
  - On D+21 the tech said "four weeks, five" before he stops or guesses; on D+28 he said "less".
  - The machine is on cycles only; pictures are taken only if they would change treatment.
  - Senna's next cycles: D+32, D+43, then about D+54.
- **Reviews:** Callie's and Tilda's household on D+41; Jab's household on D+42.
- **Rook:** his four-season debt continues; half his teaching hours count against it.
- **Wyck:** re-strapped on D+14; Keel says six weeks, about D+56.
- **Jab:** the Director's medical-register offer is standing ("I'll ask again"); Jab declined while unwell.
- **The Sag:** arrivals of 11, 14, 19 and 23 a week. The day holder is out for the winter at least, so Rook is the only holder.
- **Caul Hill:**
  - Named by Senna: six days north then west on the old line (red in Pell's atlas); a hill with its top taken off; eleven miles of fence; instrument rooms of racks bearing the same mark; hundreds of boxes thrown on the floor in 2130, "heavy and warm".
  - The Director reported the sky "wrong" over "the old plant" since the summer, with unlifted ash lying in drifts.
  - Not launched. No mission is proposed on the page.

## Bodies at exit

- **Tonk:**
  - The infection is clear (D+16). The growth plate is worse than first said, so a higher boot and a mid-growth operation are more likely.
  - The frame stays until spring.
  - He first sat up by himself on the bar on D+25, while Jab was unconscious.
- **Tilda:**
  - The pills help her mornings. She found "thistle" and loses words for about 3–4 s.
  - Her right hand drops things, and her right leg gave way on D+13 and D+20.
  - She has had no fits since the 41-second one.
  - She works the weighbridge tally left-handed by her own choice and spoke for her own household.
  - She gets no four-week picture.
- **Senna:**
  - Both hands and both feet are numb; she has to be told where the floor is.
  - Course and cycles continue.
  - She is the yard's reasons-keeper, dictating to Toren, and named Caul Hill by her own choice.
- **The day holder:** her spleen was removed (Sowerby had her open by the ninth hour on D+25). She will live. She won't hold this winter, maybe not again. Her coat was returned on the Director's order at Jab's request.
- **Rook:** held the whole bottom alone for 31 minutes on D+25 and is flat. His hands were stilled for a while by Dee's quarter-Handful on D+26. Sowerby is furious with him.
- **Toren:**
  - The left forearm is still splinted and no worse.
  - The right palm seam reopened on D+25 and was stitched.
  - Stone warm; floor measured once at 2¼ turns dark.
  - Guard: a correction he can retry ("from the ground up; soft behind"), holding for one breath unloaded.
  - His spike light has held through one fall.
- **Callie:**
  - Healing ribs, re-aggravated.
  - Nosebleeds on D+20 and D+25/26.
  - Her floor is kept, a finger above it.
  - Two lost wage days on the sheet (D+20, D+25).
  - Her handoff gap is two; her Ward walks two steps; her one-person widening lasts 11 s.
- **Jab:**
  - Went through his floor on D+25 and cannot find the bucket at all as of D+28.
  - His hands shake, and he went home against advice.
  - Publicly known ("SUSSTAIN" on the board; a mother came asking for him).
- **Wyck:** collarbone re-strapped D+14; not cleared; teaching the body lane.
- **Dessa:** her leg is bad on slopes. She learned that thirty feet is not the bottom of a dip.
- **The weighbridge girl:** broken wrist, set by Keel; fell correctly.
- **Otto:** the forearm that was stitched is itching as it heals.

## Deliberately absent or withheld

- **Paid off later, not here:** Guard (only an accident, a failed attempt and a one-breath standing test); group-scale Ward (one person for 11 s only); radiation recall (not taught; implied only through Senna's history and Jab's arithmetic); the Sustain-echo creature; the disk (not mentioned); Cinder material (only Dee's oblique line that the Homura survivors had heard what Rook was doing); the Caul Hill expedition.
- **Katori's story:** Katori's "why the same word" is untouched. Dee recognising Vera in Katori's face is planted in a single silent beat (Dee looks, Rook nods, Callie infers and says nothing).
- **Nothing named:** Dee's door count, Rook's third door, and all Director, trainee, day-holder, technician and long-coat names.
- **No romance.**
- **Milo's grief** is carried by the bread, Senna's nightly naming, Tonk joining it, and the ledger's first line. No grief speech is declared resolved.

- **Length:** 62,793 words against the packet's approximate 48,000–55,000, about 14% over the upper bound. No scene was padded and none was compressed to fit.
- **Viewpoint share, Movement Two:** Toren 37.7%, Callie 38.9%, Jab 23.4%.
- **Cumulative through Movement Two** (Movement One shares applied to its 39,707 words): Callie about 38%, Toren about 33%, Jab about 29%.
- **Viewpoint balance:** Movement Two was meant to lean toward Toren. By chapter count it does (three chapters, two of them long action or pivot chapters), but by word count Callie is slightly ahead. Jab's two chapters are the movement's two Sustain pivots. The map's Jab-leaning Movement Three would rebalance.
